29{las kyi rdo rje}55{las kyi rdo rje/}
43nā. = {'phrin las rdo rje} karmavajraḥ, ācāryaḥ śa.ko.14.
26{dwags po lha rje'i slob ma dus gsum mkhyen pa nas brgyud pa'i bka' brgyud/}28The Karma Kagyud Tradition of Tibetan Buddhism; one of the many lineages of Kagyud traditions founded by the first Karmapa Dusum Khyenpa (1110-1193), who was a distinguished disciple of Gampopa (see {sgam po pa}). He established Tsurphu Monastery, the main seat of his tradition, in the north-west of Lhasa. In India, the main centre of Karma Kagyud has been re-established in Rumtek, Sikkim.29{mnyam med dwags po lha rje la slob ma'i gtso bo khams pa mi gsum byung ba'i nang nas karma pa dus gsum mkhyen pa nas brgyud pa'i bka' brgyud chos lugs pa karma bka' brgyud zer/ming gzhan mtshur phur bka' brgyud ces kyang 'bod srol 'dug /de ni karma pa dus gsum mkhyen pas rab byung gsum pa'i sa bya (1189)lor stod lung nang du mtshur phu'i dgon pa btab ste gnas gzhi bzung bar mdzad pa nas ming de ltar grags}47{dwags po lha rje'i slob ma dus gsum mkhyen pa nas brgyud pa'i bka' brgyud/}51{dwags po bka' brgyud kyi nang gses shig ste/ mnyam med dwags po lha rje'i slob ma karma pa dus gsum mkhyen pa las brgyud pa'i bka' brgyud pa'i grub mtha' zhig}55{dwags po lha rje'i slob ma dus gsum mkhyen pa nas brgyud pa'i bka' brgyud/}

36<person> Karmapa Chödrak Gyatso37<person> Karmapa Chödrak Gyatso: As the seventh Karmapa, Chödrak Gyatso (1454–1506) was the head of the Karma Kagyü school. He was an accomplished practitioner and a prolific scholar who spent much of his life in retreat. He was nevertheless very socially engaged and worked to put an end to military conflicts, finance bridge construction, instruct people to give up hunting and fishing, and restore Buddhist iconography, specifically the central Buddha statues at Bodhgaya and Tshurpu. (Original glossary entry: https://84000.co/glossary/entity-44783.html)

shog grangs 34can zhig 'dug kyang kho bos zhib par mjal ba'i go skabs ma thob}36<person> Karma Pakṣi37<person> Karma Pakṣi: Karma Pakṣi (1204–83) was second in the line of Karmapa incarnations. His recognition as the reincarnation of the first Karmapa, Dusum Khyenpa (1110–93), is regarded as the beginning of the tulku tradition in Tibet.
